I still feel like I’m choosing what I do, like when I’m driving or changing something in the route system.\n\nBut the part that bothers me is it feels like once I do something, they already know, or they’re watching it so closely that it’s almost like I’m not being left alone to just do my job. Dispatch and compliance seem to track my route changes through the computer, and then these comments come over the radio that sound innocent, but they line up with what I just did. And then I start second-guessing myself, like, okay, who saw that, who reported it, which driver was near me, did compliance flag it?\n\nSo not controlled in the sense that someone is controlling my body or my thoughts, no. It’s more like I’m being monitored and managed without them admitting it, and certain drivers are feeding things back to dispatch. That makes me feel… not free, I guess, even if I’m technically the one doing the actions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strange or unusual beliefs that others around you don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why I’m here, because other people don’t seem to see it the way I do. They keep saying it’s normal oversight, like dispatch and compliance are just monitoring everyone’s route changes through the computer system, and I understand that on one level. I know they have systems and logs and GPS and whatever else.\n\nBut what I’m saying is it feels more specific than that. I’ll make a route change or do something in the system, and then not long after, there’ll be some little comment over the radio. Not direct, not like, “We saw you do that,” but something that lines up too closely with what I just did. And if it happened once, okay, maybe coincidence. But when it keeps happening, it’s hard to just brush it off.\n\nAnd certain drivers… I really do think some of them are reporting things back. Like they notice where I am or what I’m doing, and then it gets passed up to dispatch or compliance. People tell me I’m reading into it, or that everyone gets tracked, but I can tell there’s more going on with me specifically. That’s the part other people don’t seem to believe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might consider extremely strange or not based in reality, such as thinking that a specific system or group is deliberately targeting you in a way that goes beyond normal workplace monitoring?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I guess if you put it that way, that’s what people would say is strange. I don’t think of it as some wild thing, because to me it’s based on what keeps happening at work. But other people keep telling me I’m taking normal workplace monitoring too personally.\n\nThe reason I’m here is because dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments happen over the radio. They’ll sound casual, like just regular chatter, but they line up with what I just did or changed. And certain drivers seem to be part of it too, like they notice where I am or what I’m doing and report it back.\n\nI know they monitor everyone to some degree, but this feels different. It feels directed at me. And when I try to explain it, people act like I’m being unreasonable, but I can tell there’s more going on than just normal oversight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt like systems, people, or technology were influencing or controlling things in your life in ways others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. And I know people keep saying, “That’s just normal oversight, everyone gets monitored,” but it doesn’t feel like just general monitoring to me. It feels directed. Certain drivers seem like they notice me or what I’m doing and then report it back somehow.\n\nSo outside of work, I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where the TV is controlling me or my phone is making me do things, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the work system, the radio, dispatch, compliance, and some of the drivers all feel connected in this way that other people don’t seem to see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know other people would think is completely impossible or makes no sense, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely impossible,” because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen, and honestly it feels like it is happening. But I know other people hear me say it and they look at me like it makes no sense.\n\nLike, I’m here because of the whole thing with dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system. I’ll change something, or adjust where I’m going, and then shortly after that there’ll be some little comment over the radio. Not an obvious one, not something anyone could point to and say, “Yes, that was about you,” but it lines up too closely for me to ignore.\n\nAnd people keep telling me, “That’s just normal oversight, they monitor everyone,” and I understand that’s what they say. I understand there are systems and GPS and logs and all that. But it feels more directed than that. It feels like certain drivers are noticing what I’m doing and reporting it back, and then dispatch or compliance responds in this indirect way.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do believe something other people think doesn’t make sense. I can hear how it sounds when I say it out loud. But when I’m actually at work and it keeps happening, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one specific instance where you felt your route change was monitored or reported in a way that felt unusual or targeted toward you?\n\nPatient: One example that really stuck with me was a day when I had to change part of my route in the system because of traffic and timing. It wasn’t anything dramatic, just one of those adjustments you make so you don’t fall behind. I entered it into the computer like normal, and I remember thinking, okay, this should just be logged like everything else.\n\nBut then not long after that, maybe a few minutes, there was a comment over the radio. It wasn’t direct, like they didn’t say my name, and that’s part of why it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m reading too much into it. It was phrased like casual chatter, something about people “changing things up” or “not sticking to the plan,” and the timing was just too close to when I had made the change.\n\nAnd then one of the other drivers, someone who had been near my area earlier, said something that sounded like they were agreeing or confirming it. Again, not my name, not “she did this,” but the way they said it made me feel like they knew exactly what I had done. Like they were watching the route changes and then talking around it instead of saying it plainly.\n\nI know if you asked dispatch, they’d probably say, “We monitor routes, that’s normal,” and I understand that part. I’m not saying no one should ever see where the vehicles are. But this felt different because it was like the computer change went in, then dispatch or compliance noticed, and then the radio comments started, with certain drivers kind of playing along or reporting on me. That’s what made it feel targeted toward me, not just routine monitoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences outside of work where you felt like people, systems, or technology were monitoring or targeting you in a similar way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really in the same kind of way. I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where I think people in my neighborhood or random systems are after me. It’s more that the work situation kind of colors how I think about things afterward.\n\nThe part I keep coming back to is dispatch and compliance. I know people tell me, “They’re supposed to monitor routes, that’s their job,” and I get that on one level. I’m not saying there shouldn’t be GPS or logs or someone checking where we are. But what bothers me is when I make a route change in the computer, and then shortly after there’s some little radio comment that seems connected to it. It’s not direct enough that someone else would say, “That was obviously about you,” but to me the timing and the wording feel too specific.\n\nAnd then certain drivers seem like they’re involved too, like they notice where I am or what I changed and report it back somehow, or they say things over the radio that sound like they’re confirming it without saying my name. Other people don’t seem to agree with me about that part. They act like I’m reading into normal chatter or normal oversight, but when you’re the one it’s happening around, it feels different. It feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would consider completely implausible, like thoughts being implanted through technology or being monitored in a way that isn't possible with normal systems?\n\nPatient: No, not like thoughts being implanted or anything like that. That’s the part other people don’t seem to believe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might consider extremely strange or not based in reality, such as thinking that a specific system or group is deliberately targeting you in a way that goes beyond normal workplace monitoring?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I guess if you put it that way, that’s what people would say is strange. I don’t think of it as some wild thing, because to me it’s based on what keeps happening at work. But other people keep telling me I’m taking normal workplace monitoring too personally.\n\nThe reason I’m here is because dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments happen over the radio. They’ll sound casual, like just regular chatter, but they line up with what I just did or changed. And certain drivers seem to be part of it too, like they notice where I am or what I’m doing and report it back.\n\nI know they monitor everyone to some degree, but this feels different. It feels directed at me. And when I try to explain it, people act like I’m being unreasonable, but I can tell there’s more going on than just normal oversight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt like systems, people, or technology were influencing or controlling things in your life in ways others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, I can get uneasy with technology in general now because of what’s been happening at work, but the actual thing I’m talking about is mostly tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’m here because when I make route changes through the computer system, it feels like they’re tracking those changes more closely than they admit, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with what I just did. And I know people keep saying, “That’s just normal oversight, everyone gets monitored,” but it doesn’t feel like just general monitoring to me. It feels directed. Certain drivers seem like they notice me or what I’m doing and then report it back somehow.\n\nSo outside of work, I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where the TV is controlling me or my phone is making me do things, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the work system, the radio, dispatch, compliance, and some of the drivers all feel connected in this way that other people don’t seem to see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know other people would think is completely impossible or makes no sense, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely impossible,” because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen, and honestly it feels like it is happening. But I know other people hear me say it and they look at me like it makes no sense.\n\nLike, I’m here because of the whole thing with dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system. I’ll change something, or adjust where I’m going, and then shortly after that there’ll be some little comment over the radio. Not an obvious one, not something anyone could point to and say, “Yes, that was about you,” but it lines up too closely for me to ignore.\n\nAnd people keep telling me, “That’s just normal oversight, they monitor everyone,” and I understand that’s what they say. I understand there are systems and GPS and logs and all that. But it feels more directed than that. It feels like certain drivers are noticing what I’m doing and reporting it back, and then dispatch or compliance responds in this indirect way.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do believe something other people think doesn’t make sense. I can hear how it sounds when I say it out loud. But when I’m actually at work and it keeps happening, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one specific instance where you felt your route change was monitored or reported in a way that felt unusual or targeted toward you?\n\nPatient: One example that really stuck with me was a day when I had to change part of my route in the system because of traffic and timing. It wasn’t anything dramatic, just one of those adjustments you make so you don’t fall behind. I entered it into the computer like normal, and I remember thinking, okay, this should just be logged like everything else.\n\nBut then not long after that, maybe a few minutes, there was a comment over the radio. It wasn’t direct, like they didn’t say my name, and that’s part of why it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m reading too much into it. It was phrased like casual chatter, something about people “changing things up” or “not sticking to the plan,” and the timing was just too close to when I had made the change.\n\nAnd then one of the other drivers, someone who had been near my area earlier, said something that sounded like they were agreeing or confirming it. Again, not my name, not “she did this,” but the way they said it made me feel like they knew exactly what I had done. Like they were watching the route changes and then talking around it instead of saying it plainly.\n\nI know if you asked dispatch, they’d probably say, “We monitor routes, that’s normal,” and I understand that part. I’m not saying no one should ever see where the vehicles are. But this felt different because it was like the computer change went in, then dispatch or compliance noticed, and then the radio comments started, with certain drivers kind of playing along or reporting on me. That’s what made it feel targeted toward me, not just routine monitoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences outside of work where you felt like people, systems, or technology were monitoring or targeting you in a similar way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really in the same kind of way. I mean, I notice myself being more on edge in general now, like if something reminds me of the route system or the radio, I’ll think about it, but I don’t have the same feeling that, say, random people at the store are tracking me or that my personal phone is doing something to me.\n\nThe thing that feels real to me is still connected to work. It’s dispatch, compliance, the computer system where I put in route changes, and then the radio comments afterward. That’s where it happens. That’s where it feels like I make a change and then someone sees it, and then it gets talked about indirectly, like they’re not saying my name but they’re making it obvious enough for me to know. And certain drivers seem to be part of it, like they’re noticing where I am or what I changed and reporting it back somehow.\n\nSo if you mean outside of work completely, no, not in a separate way. But it does kind of follow me mentally, because after dealing with it at work I’ll go home and replay it, wondering who saw what, who said what, whether the comment was really about me. But the actual monitoring or targeting feeling is mostly around the work system and the people connected to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences outside of work where you felt like people or systems were targeting you in a way that others around you didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work in the same concrete way. I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where I think people in my neighborhood or random systems are after me. It’s more that the work situation kind of colors how I think about things afterward.\n\nThe part I keep coming back to is dispatch and compliance. I know people tell me, “They’re supposed to monitor routes, that’s their job,” and I get that on one level. I’m not saying there shouldn’t be GPS or logs or someone checking where we are. But what bothers me is when I make a route change in the computer, and then shortly after there’s some little radio comment that seems connected to it. It’s not direct enough that someone else would say, “That was obviously about you,” but to me the timing and the wording feel too specific.\n\nAnd then certain drivers seem like they’re involved too, like they notice where I am or what I changed and report it back somehow, or they say things over the radio that sound like they’re confirming it without saying my name. Other people don’t seem to agree with me about that part. They act like I’m reading into normal chatter or normal oversight, but when you’re the one it’s happening around, it feels different. It feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would consider completely implausible, like thoughts being implanted through technology or being monitored in a way that isn't possible with normal systems?\n\nPatient: No, not like thoughts being implanted or anything like that. I don’t think there’s some technology putting ideas in my head, or some impossible system watching me through walls or something.\n\nWhat I do believe, and I know people keep saying it’s just normal work oversight, is that dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. That part isn’t impossible, because the system does record what we do. But it feels like it goes further than just recording it. Like I’ll make a change, and then there are these little comments on the radio afterward, not saying my name directly, but close enough in timing and wording that I can tell it’s about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers seem to be part of it too. Like they’re noticing where I am or what I changed and reporting on me somehow, or playing along with the comments. So I wouldn’t say I believe in something completely impossible. I’m saying the way they’re using the normal systems at work feels targeted toward me, even if other people act like I’m overthinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that felt completely real to you, but that you later found out were not true or were impossible?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “impossible,” exactly. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on, because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen through the normal systems at work, you know? Dispatch can see route changes. Compliance can look at the computer system. The radio is there. Drivers talk. So when people say, “That’s not happening,” I don’t hear it as impossible, I hear it as they’re not paying attention to the same pattern I’m seeing.\n\nBut yes, I guess there have been times where something felt completely real to me and other people later told me I was wrong about it. Like I’ll make a change in the system, even something small, and then a little while later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about exactly that. Not my name, not direct, but specific enough that I know they’re referring to me. And then if I ask someone else, or mention it, they’ll say it was just normal radio chatter or just dispatch doing their job. But it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nSo I can’t honestly say I found out it was impossible, because I still don’t think it is impossible. But I have had people tell me I’m reading into it, that the drivers aren’t reporting on me and dispatch isn’t singling me out. I try to consider that, I really do, but then it happens again—the timing, the wording, the same people—and it feels real all over again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

No, I don’t really have an example like that outside of the work thing. I know I keep coming back to dispatch and compliance, but that’s because that’s the situation that actually feels real to me.

It’s not like I believed some random thing and then later went, “Oh, that made no sense.” With work, it’s more complicated, because the computer system really does show route changes, and dispatch really can see things, and compliance does check stuff. It feels like they’re tracking it and talking around it.

It’s the timing, and the wording, and the same people being involved. So no, I can’t think of a separate belief that I later realized was definitely not true or nonsense. Like certain drivers are noticing things and reporting them back, and then dispatch makes these little comments so I know they know.\n\nSo not thoughts being broadcast like everyone can hear them in my head, no… but it does feel like they’re tracking me very closely, more than they admit.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like you were being watched or that others could know your actions or thoughts without you telling them?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, I get uneasy sometimes after all this started, like I’ll be thinking about it when I’m at home or driving home, and I’ll wonder if I missed something or if something I did is going to get talked about the next day. But the main thing is at work, because that’s where it actually happens.\n\nIt’s the route changes in the computer system, and then dispatch or compliance seems to know right away, and then there are these little comments over the radio that sound like they’re just normal work chatter, but they match up with what I just did too closely. And I know people say, “Well, that’s their job, they monitor routes,” but it feels like more than that. Like certain drivers are watching what I’m doing and reporting it back, and then dispatch kind of lets me know they know without saying it directly.\n\nSo outside of work, I don’t have as much proof, I guess. It’s more that the feeling follows me because I know what’s happening there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone was controlling your actions or thoughts, like you weren’t fully in control of what you were doing or thinking?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way, like someone is moving my hands or making me think something. I still feel like I’m choosing what I do, like when I’m driving or changing something in the route system.\n\nBut the part that bothers me is it feels like once I do something, they already know, or they’re watching it so closely that it’s almost like I’m not being left alone to just do my job. Dispatch and compliance seem to track my route changes through the computer, and then these comments come over the radio that sound innocent, but they line up with what I just did. And then I start second-guessing myself, like, okay, who saw that, who reported it, which driver was near me, did compliance flag it?\n\nSo not controlled in the sense that someone is controlling my body or my thoughts, no. It’s more like I’m being monitored and managed without them admitting it, and certain drivers are feeding things back to dispatch. That makes me feel… not free, I guess, even if I’m technically the one doing the actions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strange or unusual beliefs that others around you don’t seem to share, even when they’ve tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why I’m here, because other people don’t seem to see it the way I do. They keep saying it’s normal oversight, like dispatch and compliance are just monitoring everyone’s route changes through the computer system, and I understand that on one level. I know they have systems and logs and GPS and whatever else.\n\nBut what I’m saying is it feels more specific than that. I’ll make a route change or do something in the system, and then not long after, there’ll be some little comment over the radio. Not direct, not like, “We saw you do that,” but something that lines up too closely with what I just did. And if it happened once, okay, maybe coincidence. But when it keeps happening, it’s hard to just brush it off.\n\nAnd certain drivers… I really do think some of them are reporting things back. Like they notice where I am or what I’m doing, and then it gets passed up to dispatch or compliance. People tell me I’m reading into it, or that everyone gets tracked, but I can tell there’s more going on with me specifically. That’s the part other people don’t seem to believe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might consider extremely strange or not based in reality, such as thinking that a specific system or group is deliberately targeting you in a way that goes beyond normal workplace monitoring?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I guess if you put it that way, that’s what people would say is strange. I don’t think of it as some wild thing, because to me it’s based on what keeps happening at work. But other people keep telling me I’m taking normal workplace monitoring too personally.\n\nThe reason I’m here is because dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments happen over the radio. They’ll sound casual, like just regular chatter, but they line up with what I just did or changed. And certain drivers seem to be part of it too, like they notice where I am or what I’m doing and report it back.\n\nI know they monitor everyone to some degree, but this feels different. It feels directed at me. And when I try to explain it, people act like I’m being unreasonable, but I can tell there’s more going on than just normal oversight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences outside of work where you felt like systems, people, or technology were influencing or controlling things in your life in ways others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, I can get uneasy with technology in general now because of what’s been happening at work, but the actual thing I’m talking about is mostly tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’m here because when I make route changes through the computer system, it feels like they’re tracking those changes more closely than they admit, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with what I just did. And I know people keep saying, “That’s just normal oversight, everyone gets monitored,” but it doesn’t feel like just general monitoring to me. It feels directed. Certain drivers seem like they notice me or what I’m doing and then report it back somehow.\n\nSo outside of work, I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where the TV is controlling me or my phone is making me do things, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the work system, the radio, dispatch, compliance, and some of the drivers all feel connected in this way that other people don’t seem to see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know other people would think is completely impossible or makes no sense, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely impossible,” because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen, and honestly it feels like it is happening. But I know other people hear me say it and they look at me like it makes no sense.\n\nLike, I’m here because of the whole thing with dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system. I’ll change something, or adjust where I’m going, and then shortly after that there’ll be some little comment over the radio. Not an obvious one, not something anyone could point to and say, “Yes, that was about you,” but it lines up too closely for me to ignore.\n\nAnd people keep telling me, “That’s just normal oversight, they monitor everyone,” and I understand that’s what they say. I understand there are systems and GPS and logs and all that. But it feels more directed than that. It feels like certain drivers are noticing what I’m doing and reporting it back, and then dispatch or compliance responds in this indirect way.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do believe something other people think doesn’t make sense. I can hear how it sounds when I say it out loud. But when I’m actually at work and it keeps happening, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one specific instance where you felt your route change was monitored or reported in a way that felt unusual or targeted toward you?\n\nPatient: One example that really stuck with me was a day when I had to change part of my route in the system because of traffic and timing. It wasn’t anything dramatic, just one of those adjustments you make so you don’t fall behind. I entered it into the computer like normal, and I remember thinking, okay, this should just be logged like everything else.\n\nBut then not long after that, maybe a few minutes, there was a comment over the radio. It wasn’t direct, like they didn’t say my name, and that’s part of why it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m reading too much into it. It was phrased like casual chatter, something about people “changing things up” or “not sticking to the plan,” and the timing was just too close to when I had made the change.\n\nAnd then one of the other drivers, someone who had been near my area earlier, said something that sounded like they were agreeing or confirming it. Again, not my name, not “she did this,” but the way they said it made me feel like they knew exactly what I had done. Like they were watching the route changes and then talking around it instead of saying it plainly.\n\nI know if you asked dispatch, they’d probably say, “We monitor routes, that’s normal,” and I understand that part. I’m not saying no one should ever see where the vehicles are. But this felt different because it was like the computer change went in, then dispatch or compliance noticed, and then the radio comments started, with certain drivers kind of playing along or reporting on me. That’s what made it feel targeted toward me, not just routine monitoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences outside of work where you felt like people, systems, or technology were monitoring or targeting you in a similar way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really in the same kind of way. I mean, I notice myself being more on edge in general now, like if something reminds me of the route system or the radio, I’ll think about it, but I don’t have the same feeling that, say, random people at the store are tracking me or that my personal phone is doing something to me.\n\nThe thing that feels real to me is still connected to work. It’s dispatch, compliance, the computer system where I put in route changes, and then the radio comments afterward. That’s where it happens. That’s where it feels like I make a change and then someone sees it, and then it gets talked about indirectly, like they’re not saying my name but they’re making it obvious enough for me to know. And certain drivers seem to be part of it, like they’re noticing where I am or what I changed and reporting it back somehow.\n\nSo if you mean outside of work completely, no, not in a separate way. But it does kind of follow me mentally, because after dealing with it at work I’ll go home and replay it, wondering who saw what, who said what, whether the comment was really about me. But the actual monitoring or targeting feeling is mostly around the work system and the people connected to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences outside of work where you felt like people or systems were targeting you in a way that others around you didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work in the same concrete way. I don’t have, like, a whole separate thing where I think people in my neighborhood or random systems are after me. It’s more that the work situation kind of colors how I think about things afterward.\n\nThe part I keep coming back to is dispatch and compliance. I know people tell me, “They’re supposed to monitor routes, that’s their job,” and I get that on one level. I’m not saying there shouldn’t be GPS or logs or someone checking where we are. But what bothers me is when I make a route change in the computer, and then shortly after there’s some little radio comment that seems connected to it. It’s not direct enough that someone else would say, “That was obviously about you,” but to me the timing and the wording feel too specific.\n\nAnd then certain drivers seem like they’re involved too, like they notice where I am or what I changed and report it back somehow, or they say things over the radio that sound like they’re confirming it without saying my name. Other people don’t seem to agree with me about that part. They act like I’m reading into normal chatter or normal oversight, but when you’re the one it’s happening around, it feels different. It feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would consider completely implausible, like thoughts being implanted through technology or being monitored in a way that isn't possible with normal systems?\n\nPatient: No, not like thoughts being implanted or anything like that. I don’t think there’s some technology putting ideas in my head, or some impossible system watching me through walls or something.\n\nWhat I do believe, and I know people keep saying it’s just normal work oversight, is that dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. That part isn’t impossible, because the system does record what we do. But it feels like it goes further than just recording it. Like I’ll make a change, and then there are these little comments on the radio afterward, not saying my name directly, but close enough in timing and wording that I can tell it’s about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers seem to be part of it too. Like they’re noticing where I am or what I changed and reporting on me somehow, or playing along with the comments. So I wouldn’t say I believe in something completely impossible. I’m saying the way they’re using the normal systems at work feels targeted toward me, even if other people act like I’m overthinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that felt completely real to you, but that you later found out were not true or were impossible?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “impossible,” exactly. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on, because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen through the normal systems at work, you know? Dispatch can see route changes. Compliance can look at the computer system. The radio is there. Drivers talk. So when people say, “That’s not happening,” I don’t hear it as impossible, I hear it as they’re not paying attention to the same pattern I’m seeing.\n\nBut yes, I guess there have been times where something felt completely real to me and other people later told me I was wrong about it. Like I’ll make a change in the system, even something small, and then a little while later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about exactly that. Not my name, not direct, but specific enough that I know they’re referring to me. And then if I ask someone else, or mention it, they’ll say it was just normal radio chatter or just dispatch doing their job. But it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nSo I can’t honestly say I found out it was impossible, because I still don’t think it is impossible. But I have had people tell me I’m reading into it, that the drivers aren’t reporting on me and dispatch isn’t singling me out. I try to consider that, I really do, but then it happens again—the timing, the wording, the same people—and it feels real all over again.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that wasn’t related to work monitoring, and that you later realized was definitely not true or made no sense at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really have an example like that outside of the work thing. I know I keep coming back to dispatch and compliance, but that’s because that’s the situation that actually feels real to me.\n\nIt’s not like I believed some random thing and then later went, “Oh, that made no sense.” With work, it’s more complicated, because the computer system really does show route changes, and dispatch really can see things, and compliance does check stuff. So when I change something and then there’s a little radio comment afterward, or certain drivers seem to react like they already know what I did, it doesn’t feel made up to me. It feels like they’re tracking it and talking around it.\n\nOther people will say, “That’s just normal oversight,” or “Drivers talk on the radio all day,” and I understand why they say that. But I can still tell when something is aimed at me. It’s the timing, and the wording, and the same people being involved. So no, I can’t think of a separate belief that I later realized was definitely not true or nonsense. This is the main thing, and I still don’t think it makes no sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know people tell me, “They’re supposed to monitor routes, that’s their job,” and I get that on one level. I’m not saying there shouldn’t be GPS or logs or someone checking where we are. But what bothers me is when I make a route change in the computer, and then shortly after there’s some little radio comment that seems connected to it. It’s not direct enough that someone else would say, “That was obviously about you,” but to me the timing and the wording feel too specific.\n\nAnd then certain drivers seem like they’re involved too, like they notice where I am or what I changed and report it back somehow, or they say things over the radio that sound like they’re confirming it without saying my name. Other people don’t seem to agree with me about that part. They act like I’m reading into normal chatter or normal oversight, but when you’re the one it’s happening around, it feels different. It feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would consider completely implausible, like thoughts being implanted through technology or being monitored in a way that isn't possible with normal systems?\n\nPatient: No, not like thoughts being implanted or anything like that. I don’t think there’s some technology putting ideas in my head, or some impossible system watching me through walls or something.\n\nWhat I do believe, and I know people keep saying it’s just normal work oversight, is that dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. That part isn’t impossible, because the system does record what we do. But it feels like it goes further than just recording it. Like I’ll make a change, and then there are these little comments on the radio afterward, not saying my name directly, but close enough in timing and wording that I can tell it’s about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers seem to be part of it too. Like they’re noticing where I am or what I changed and reporting on me somehow, or playing along with the comments. So I wouldn’t say I believe in something completely impossible. I’m saying the way they’re using the normal systems at work feels targeted toward me, even if other people act like I’m overthinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that felt completely real to you, but that you later found out were not true or were impossible?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “impossible,” exactly. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on, because to me it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like something that could happen through the normal systems at work, you know? Dispatch can see route changes. Compliance can look at the computer system. The radio is there. Drivers talk. So when people say, “That’s not happening,” I don’t hear it as impossible, I hear it as they’re not paying attention to the same pattern I’m seeing.\n\nBut yes, I guess there have been times where something felt completely real to me and other people later told me I was wrong about it. Like I’ll make a change in the system, even something small, and then a little while later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about exactly that. Not my name, not direct, but specific enough that I know they’re referring to me. And then if I ask someone else, or mention it, they’ll say it was just normal radio chatter or just dispatch doing their job. But it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nSo I can’t honestly say I found out it was impossible, because I still don’t think it is impossible. But I have had people tell me I’m reading into it, that the drivers aren’t reporting on me and dispatch isn’t singling me out. I try to consider that, I really do, but then it happens again—the timing, the wording, the same people—and it feels real all over again.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that wasn’t related to work monitoring, and that you later realized was definitely not true or made no sense at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really have an example like that outside of the work thing. I know I keep coming back to dispatch and compliance, but that’s because that’s the situation that actually feels real to me.\n\nIt’s not like I believed some random thing and then later went, “Oh, that made no sense.” With work, it’s more complicated, because the computer system really does show route changes, and dispatch really can see things, and compliance does check stuff. So when I change something and then there’s a little radio comment afterward, or certain drivers seem to react like they already know what I did, it doesn’t feel made up to me. It feels like they’re tracking it and talking around it.\n\nOther people will say, “That’s just normal oversight,” or “Drivers talk on the radio all day,” and I understand why they say that. But I can still tell when something is aimed at me. It’s the timing, and the wording, and the same people being involved. So no, I can’t think of a separate belief that I later realized was definitely not true or nonsense. This is the main thing, and I still don’t think it makes no sense."
  }
